    1|       |/**
    2|       | *  @note This file is part of Empirical, https://github.com/devosoft/Empirical
    3|       | *  @copyright Copyright (C) Michigan State University, MIT Software license; see doc/LICENSE.md
    4|       | *  @date 2016-2020.
    5|       | *
    6|       | *  @file Ptr.hpp
    7|       | *  @brief A wrapper for pointers that does careful memory tracking (but only in debug mode).
    8|       | *  @note Status: BETA
    9|       | *
   10|       | *  Ptr objects behave as normal pointers under most conditions.  However, if a program is
   11|       | *  compiled with EMP_TRACK_MEM set, then these pointers perform extra tests to ensure that
   12|       | *  they point to valid memory and that memory is freed before pointers are released.
   13|       | *
   14|       | *  If you trip an assert, you can re-do the run a track a specific pointer by defining
   15|       | *  EMP_ABORT_PTR_NEW or EMP_ABORT_PTR_DELETE to the ID of the pointer in question.  This will
   16|       | *  allow you to track the pointer more easily in a debugger.
   17|       | *
   18|       | *  @todo Track information about emp::vector and emp::array objects to make sure we don't
   19|       | *    point directly into them? (A resize() could make such pointers invalid!) Or better, warn
   20|       | *    it vector memory could have moved.
   21|       | *  @todo Get working with threads
   22|       | */
   23|       |

  699|       |    /// Does this Ptr point to a memory position before or equal to a raw pointer?
  700|       |    bool operator<=(const TYPE * in_ptr) const { return ptr <= in_ptr; }
  701|       |
  702|       |    /// Does this Ptr point to a memory position after a raw pointer?
  703|       |    bool operator>(const TYPE * in_ptr)  const { return ptr > in_ptr; }
  704|       |
  705|       |    /// Does this Ptr point to a memory position after or equal to a raw pointer?
  706|       |    bool operator>=(const TYPE * in_ptr) const { return ptr >= in_ptr; }
  707|       |
  708|       |
  709|       |    /// Some debug testing functions
  710|       |    int DebugGetCount() const { return Tracker().GetIDCount(id); }
  711|       |    bool DebugIsArray() const { return Tracker().IsArrayID(id); }
  712|       |    size_t DebugGetArrayBytes() const { return Tracker().GetArrayBytes(id); }
  713|       |    bool DebugIsActive() const { return Tracker().IsActiveID(id); }
  714|       |
  715|       |    bool OK() const {
  716|       |      // Untracked ID's should not have pointers in the Tracker.
  717|       |      if (id == UNTRACKED_ID) return !Tracker().HasPtr(ptr);
  718|       |
  719|       |      // Make sure this pointer is linked to the correct info.
  720|       |      if (Tracker().GetInfo(id).GetPtr() != ptr) return false;
  721|       |
  722|       |      // And make sure that info itself is okay.
  723|       |      return Tracker().GetInfo(id).OK();
  724|       |    }
  725|       |
  726|       |    // Prevent use of new and delete on Ptr
  727|       |    // static void* operator new(std::size_t) noexcept {
  728|       |    //   emp_assert(false, "No Ptr::operator new; use emp::NewPtr for clarity.");
  729|       |    //   return nullptr;
  730|       |    // }

  733|       |    //   return nullptr;
  734|       |    // }
  735|       |    //

  738|       |    // }
  739|       |    // static void operator delete[](void* ptr, std::size_t sz) {
  740|       |    //   emp_assert(false, "No Ptr::operator delete[]; use DeleteArray() member function for clarity.");
  741|       |    // }
  742|       |
  743|       |  };
  744|       |
  745|       |#else // #ifdef EMP_TRACK_MEM
  746|       |
  747|       |
  748|       |  template <typename TYPE>
  749|       |  class BasePtr {
  750|       |  protected:
  751|       |    TYPE * ptr;                 ///< The raw pointer associated with this Ptr object.
  752|       |
  753|       |  public:
  754|       |    BasePtr(TYPE * in_ptr) : ptr(in_ptr) { }
  755|       |
  756|       |    // Dereference a pointer.
  757|       |    [[nodiscard]] TYPE & operator*() const { return *ptr; }
  758|       |
  759|       |    // Follow a pointer.
  760|       |    TYPE * operator->() const { return ptr; }
  761|       |
  762|       |    // Should implement operator->* to follow a pointer to a member function.
  763|       |    // For an example, see:
  764|       |    //  https://stackoverflow.com/questions/27634036/overloading-operator-in-c
  765|       |
  766|       |    // Indexing into array
  767|       |    TYPE & operator[](size_t pos) const { return ptr[pos]; }
  768|       |  };
  769|       |
  770|       |  /// Base class with functionality only needed in void pointers.
  771|       |  template <> class BasePtr<void> {
  772|       |  protected: void * ptr;                 ///< The raw pointer associated with this Ptr object.
  773|       |  public: BasePtr(void * in_ptr) : ptr(in_ptr) { }
  774|       |  };
  775|       |
  776|       |  template <> class BasePtr<const void> {
  777|       |  protected: const void * ptr;           ///< The raw pointer associated with this Ptr object.
  778|       |  public: BasePtr(const void * in_ptr) : ptr(in_ptr) { }
  779|       |  };
  780|       |
  781|       |  template <typename TYPE>
  782|       |  class Ptr : public BasePtr<TYPE> {
  783|       |  private:
  784|       |    using BasePtr<TYPE>::ptr;
  785|       |
  786|       |  public:
  787|       |    using element_type = TYPE;
  788|       |
  789|       |    /// Default constructor
  790|       |    Ptr() : BasePtr<TYPE>(nullptr) {}
  791|       |
  792|       |    /// Copy constructor
  793|       |    Ptr(const Ptr<TYPE> & _in) : BasePtr<TYPE>(_in.ptr) {}
  794|       |
  795|       |    /// Construct from raw ptr
  796|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr(T2 * in_ptr, bool=false) : BasePtr<TYPE>(in_ptr) {}
  797|       |
  798|       |    /// Construct from array
  799|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr(T2 * _ptr, size_t, bool) : BasePtr<TYPE>(_ptr) {}
  800|       |
  801|       |    /// From compatible Ptr
  802|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr(Ptr<T2> _in) : BasePtr<TYPE>(_in.Raw()) {}
  803|       |
  804|       |    /// From nullptr
  805|       |    Ptr(std::nullptr_t) : Ptr() {}
  806|       |
  807|       |    /// Destructor
  808|       |    ~Ptr() { ; }
  809|       |
  810|       |    [[nodiscard]] bool IsNull() const { return ptr == nullptr; }
  811|       |    [[nodiscard]] TYPE * Raw() const { return ptr; }
  812|       |    [[nodiscard]] TYPE * Raw(size_t pos) const { return &(ptr[pos]); }
  813|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr<T2> Cast() const { return (T2*) ptr; }
  814|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr<T2> ConstCast() const { return const_cast<T2*>(ptr); }
  815|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr<T2> DynamicCast() const { return dynamic_cast<T2*>(ptr); }
  816|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr<T2> ReinterpretCast() const { return reinterpret_cast<T2*>(ptr); }
  817|       |
  818|       |    template <typename... T>
  819|       |    void New(T &&... args) { ptr = new TYPE(std::forward<T>(args)...); }  // New raw pointer.
  820|       |    void NewArray(size_t array_size) { ptr = new TYPE[array_size]; }
  821|       |    void Delete() { delete ptr; }
  822|       |    void DeleteArray() { delete [] ptr; }
  823|       |
  824|       |    size_t Hash() const {
  825|       |      static constexpr size_t shift = internal::Log2(1 + sizeof(TYPE));  // Chop off useless bits...
  826|       |      return (size_t)(ptr) >> shift;
  827|       |    }
  828|       |    struct hash_t { size_t operator()(const Ptr<TYPE> & t) const { return t.Hash(); } };
  829|       |
  830|       |    // Copy assignments
  831|       |    Ptr<TYPE> & operator=(const Ptr<TYPE> & _in) { ptr = _in.ptr; return *this; }
  832|       |
  833|       |    // Assign to compatible Ptr or raw (non-managed) pointer.
  834|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr<TYPE> & operator=(T2 * _in) { ptr = _in; return *this; }
  835|       |    template <typename T2> Ptr<TYPE> & operator=(Ptr<T2> _in) { ptr = _in.Raw(); return *this; }
  836|       |
  837|       |    // Auto-cast to raw pointer type.
  838|       |    operator TYPE *() { return ptr; }
  839|       |
  840|       |    operator bool() { return ptr != nullptr; }
  841|       |    operator bool() const { return ptr != nullptr; }
  842|       |
  843|       |    // Comparisons to other Ptr objects
  844|       |    bool operator==(const Ptr<TYPE> & in_ptr) const { return ptr == in_ptr.ptr; }
  845|       |    bool operator!=(const Ptr<TYPE> & in_ptr) const { return ptr != in_ptr.ptr; }
  846|       |    bool operator<(const Ptr<TYPE> & in_ptr)  const { return ptr < in_ptr.ptr; }
  847|       |    bool operator<=(const Ptr<TYPE> & in_ptr) const { return ptr <= in_ptr.ptr; }
  848|       |    bool operator>(const Ptr<TYPE> & in_ptr)  const { return ptr > in_ptr.ptr; }
  849|       |    bool operator>=(const Ptr<TYPE> & in_ptr) const { return ptr >= in_ptr.ptr; }
  850|       |
  851|       |    // Comparisons to raw pointers.
  852|       |    bool operator==(const TYPE * in_ptr) const { return ptr == in_ptr; }
  853|       |    bool operator!=(const TYPE * in_ptr) const { return ptr != in_ptr; }
  854|       |    bool operator<(const TYPE * in_ptr)  const { return ptr < in_ptr; }
  855|       |    bool operator<=(const TYPE * in_ptr) const { return ptr <= in_ptr; }
  856|       |    bool operator>(const TYPE * in_ptr)  const { return ptr > in_ptr; }
  857|       |    bool operator>=(const TYPE * in_ptr) const { return ptr >= in_ptr; }
  858|       |
  859|       |    // Stubs for debug-related functions when outside debug mode.
  860|       |    int DebugGetCount() const { return -1; }
  861|       |    bool DebugIsArray() const { emp_assert(false); return false; }
  862|       |    size_t DebugGetArrayBytes() const { return 0; }
  863|       |    bool DebugIsActive() const { return true; }
  864|       |    bool OK() const { return true; }
  865|       |  };
  866|       |
  867|       |#endif // #ifdef EMP_TRACK_MEM
  868|       |

  870|       |  template <typename T>
  871|      0|  std::ostream & operator<<(std::ostream & out, const emp::Ptr<T> & ptr) {
  872|      0|    out << ptr.Raw();

  876|       |  // @CAO: Reading a pointer from a stream seems like a terrible idea in most situations, but I
  877|       |  // can imagine limited circumstances where it would be needed.
  878|       |  template <typename T, typename... Ts>
  879|       |  std::istream & operator>>(std::istream & is, emp::Ptr<T> & ptr) {
  880|       |    T * val;
  881|       |    is >> val;
  882|       |    ptr = val;
  883|       |    return is;
  884|       |  }
  885|       |
  886|       |  /// Convert a T* to a Ptr<T>.  By default, don't track.
  887|       |  template <typename T>
  888|       |  [[nodiscard]] Ptr<T> ToPtr(T * _in, bool own=false) { return Ptr<T>(_in, own); }
  889|       |
  890|       |  /// Convert a T* to a Ptr<T> that we DO track.
  891|       |  template <typename T>
  892|       |  [[nodiscard]] Ptr<T> TrackPtr(T * _in, bool own=true) { return Ptr<T>(_in, own); }
  893|       |
  894|       |  /// Create a new Ptr of the target type; use the args in the constructor.
  895|       |  template <typename T, typename... ARGS>
  896|       |  [[nodiscard]] Ptr<T> NewPtr(ARGS &&... args) {
  897|       |    auto ptr = new T(std::forward<ARGS>(args)...);
  898|       |    // auto ptr = (T*) malloc (sizeof(T));         // Build a new raw pointer.
  899|       |    // emp_assert(ptr);                            // No exceptions in emscripten; assert alloc!
  900|       |    // new (ptr) T(std::forward<ARGS>(args)...);   // Special new that uses allocated space.
  901|       |    return Ptr<T>(ptr, true);
  902|       |  }
  903|       |
  904|       |  /// Copy an object pointed to and return a Ptr to the copy.
  905|       |  template <typename T>
  906|       |  [[nodiscard]] Ptr<T> CopyPtr(Ptr<T> in) { return NewPtr<T>(*in); }
  907|       |
  908|       |  /// Copy a vector of objects pointed to; return a vector of Ptrs to the new copies.
  909|       |  template <typename T>
  910|       |  [[nodiscard]] emp::vector<Ptr<T>> CopyPtrs(const emp::vector<Ptr<T>> & in) {
  911|       |    emp::vector<Ptr<T>> out_ptrs(in.size());
  912|       |    for (size_t i = 0; i < in.size(); i++) out_ptrs[i] = CopyPtr(in[i]);
  913|       |    return out_ptrs;
  914|       |  }
  915|       |
  916|       |  /// Copy a vector of objects pointed to by using their Clone() member function; return vector.
  917|       |  template <typename T>
  918|       |  [[nodiscard]] emp::vector<Ptr<T>> ClonePtrs(const emp::vector<Ptr<T>> & in) {
  919|       |    emp::vector<Ptr<T>> out_ptrs(in.size());
  920|       |    for (size_t i = 0; i < in.size(); i++) out_ptrs[i] = in[i]->Clone();
  921|       |    return out_ptrs;
  922|       |  }
  923|       |
  924|       |  /// Create a pointer to an array of objects.
  925|       |  template <typename T, typename... ARGS>
  926|       |  [[nodiscard]] Ptr<T> NewArrayPtr(size_t array_size, ARGS &&... args) {
  927|       |    auto ptr = new T[array_size];                     // Build a new raw pointer.
  928|       |    // const size_t alloc_size = array_size * sizeof(T);
  929|       |    // auto ptr = (T*) malloc (alloc_size);
  930|       |    emp_assert(ptr, array_size);                      // No exceptions in emscripten; assert alloc!
  931|       |    // for (size_t i = 0; i < array_size; i++) {         // Loop through all array elements.
  932|       |    //   new (ptr + i*sizeof(T)) T(args...);             //    ...and initialize them.
  933|       |    // }
  934|       |    return Ptr<T>(ptr, array_size, true);
  935|       |  }

    1|       |/**
    2|       | *  @note This file is part of Empirical, https://github.com/devosoft/Empirical
    3|       | *  @copyright Copyright (C) Michigan State University, MIT Software license; see doc/LICENSE.md
    4|       | *  @date 2020.
    5|       | *
    6|       | *  @file always_assert.hpp
    7|       | *  @brief A more dynamic replacement for standard library asserts.
    8|       | *  @note Status: RELEASE
    9|       | *
   10|       | *  A replacement for the system-level assert.h, called "emp_always_assert"
   11|       | *  Added functionality:
   12|       | *   - If compiled with Emscripten, will provide pop-up alerts in a web browser.
   13|       | *   - emp_assert can take additional arguments.  If the assert is triggered,
   14|       | *     those extra arguments will be evaluated and printed.
   15|       | *   - if EMP_TDEBUG is defined, emp_assert() goes into test mode and records
   16|       | *     failures, but does not abort.  (useful for unit tests of asserts)
   17|       | *
   18|       | *  Example:
   19|       | *
   20|       | *     int a = 6;
   21|       | *     emp_always_assert(a==5, a);
   22|       | *
   23|       | *  Unlinke "emp_assert", "emp_always_assert" will trigger an assertion error
   24|       | *  whether compiled in debug mode or not.
   25|       | *
   26|       | */
   27|       |

   29|       |#define EMP_ALWAYS_ASSERT_HPP

    6|       | *  @file assert.hpp
    7|       | *  @brief A more dynamic replacement for standard library asserts.
    8|       | *  @note Status: RELEASE
    9|       | *
   10|       | *  A replacement for the system-level assert.h, called "emp_assert"
   11|       | *  Added functionality:
   12|       | *   - If compiled with Emscripten, will provide pop-up alerts in a web browser.
   13|       | *   - emp_assert can take additional arguments.  If the assert is triggered, those extra
   14|       | *     arguments will be evaluated and printed.
   15|       | *   - if NDEBUG -or- EMP_NDEBUG is defined, the expression in emp_assert() is not evaluated.
   16|       | *   - if EMP_TDEBUG is defined, emp_assert() goes into test mode and records failures, but
   17|       | *     does not abort.  (useful for unit tests of asserts)
   18|       | *
   19|       | *  Example:
   20|       | *
   21|       | *     int a = 6;
   22|       | *     emp_assert(a==5, a);
   23|       | *
   24|       | *  When compiled in debug mode (i.e. without the -DNDEBUG flag), this will
   25|       | *  trigger an assertion error and print the value of a.
   26|       | */

  316|       |  /// Retrieve a vector of TypeIDs for a pack of types passed in.
  317|       |  template <typename... Ts>
  318|       |  emp::vector<TypeID> GetTypeIDs() {
  319|       |    return emp::vector<TypeID>{GetTypeID<Ts>()...};
  320|       |  }
  321|       |
  322|       |  #ifndef DOXYGEN_SHOULD_SKIP_THIS
  323|       |  namespace internal {
  324|       |    // Unimplemented base class -- TypePackIDs are meaningless for non-TypePack classes.
  325|       |    template <typename T> struct TypePackIDs_impl { };
  326|       |
  327|       |    // Decompose a TypePack and provide a vector of the individual TypeIDs.
  328|       |    template <typename... Ts>
  329|       |    struct TypePackIDs_impl<TypePack<Ts...>> {
  330|       |      static emp::vector<TypeID> GetIDs() { return GetTypeIDs<Ts...>(); }
  331|       |    };
  332|       |  }
  333|       |  #endif // DOXYGEN_SHOULD_SKIP_THIS
  334|       |
  335|       |  /// Retrieve a vector of TypeIDs for a TypePack of types passed in.
  336|       |  template <typename T>
  337|       |  emp::vector<TypeID> GetTypePackIDs() {
  338|       |    return internal::TypePackIDs_impl<T>::GetIDs();
  339|       |  }
  340|       |
  341|       |  /// Build the information for a single TypeID.
  342|       |  template <typename T>
  343|     16|  static emp::Ptr<TypeID::Info> BuildInfo() {
  344|     16|    static TypeID::InfoData<T> info;
  345|     16|    if (info.init == false) {
  346|     16|      TypeID type_id(&info);
  347|       |
  348|     16|      info.init = true;
  349|     16|      info.name = typeid(T).name();
  350|       |
  351|       |      // Now, fix the name if we can be more precise about it.
  352|     16|      if (info.IsConst()) {
  353|      0|        info.name = "const "s + type_id.GetRemoveConstTypeID().GetName();
  354|      0|      }
  355|     16|      else if (info.IsVolatile()) {
  356|      0|        info.name = "volatile "s + type_id.GetRemoveVolatileTypeID().GetName();
  357|      0|      }
  358|     16|      else if (info.IsPointer()) {
  359|      0|        info.name = type_id.GetRemovePointerTypeID().GetName() + '*';
  360|      0|      }
  361|     16|      else if (info.IsReference()) {
  362|      0|        info.name = type_id.GetRemoveReferenceTypeID().GetName() + '&';
  363|      0|      }
  364|     16|      else if constexpr (emp::is_TypePack<T>()) {
  365|     16|        emp::vector<TypeID> ids = GetTypePackIDs<T>();
  366|     16|        info.name = "TypePack<";
  367|     16|        for (size_t i = 0; i < ids.size(); i++) {
  368|     16|          if (i) info.name += ",";
  369|     16|          info.name += ids[i].GetName();
  370|     16|        }
  371|     16|        info.name += ">";
  372|     16|      }
  373|     16|    }
  374|       |
  375|     16|    return &info;
  376|     16|  }

/home/runner/workspace/third-party/Empirical/include/emp/meta/TypePack.hpp:
    1|       |/**
    2|       | *  @note This file is part of Empirical, https://github.com/devosoft/Empirical
    3|       | *  @copyright Copyright (C) Michigan State University, MIT Software license; see doc/LICENSE.md
    4|       | *  @date 2016-2018
    5|       | *
    6|       | *  @file  TypePack.hpp
    7|       | *  @brief A set of types that can be manipulated at compile time (good for metaprogramming)
    8|       | *
    9|       | *  TypePacks are static structues that provide a large set of mechanisms to access and adjust
   10|       | *  the included types.
   11|       | *
   12|       | *  To create a typepack, just pass in zero or more types into the TypePack template.
   13|       | *
   14|       | *    using my_pack = emp::TypePack<int, std::string, double>;
   15|       | *
   16|       | *  After manipulations, you can apply a type pack using the apply<> member template.  E.g.,
   17|       | *
   18|       | *    my_pack::resize<5,char>::reverse::apply<std::tuple> my_tuple;
   19|       | *
   20|       | *  ...would create a variable of type std::tuple<char, char, double, std::string, int>.
   21|       | *
   22|       | *
   23|       | *  Member functions include (all of which are constexpr):
   24|       | *    Has<T>()           - Return true/false: Is T is part of the pack?
   25|       | *    Count<T>()         - Return number of times T is in the pack.
   26|       | *    GetID<T>()         - Return first position of T in the pack, (or -1 if none).
   27|       | *    GetSize()          - Return total number of types in this pack.
   28|       | *    IsEmpty()          - Return true/false: Is this pack empty?
   29|       | *    IsUnique()         - Return true/false: are all types in pack are distinct?
   30|       | *
   31|       | *  Type accessors:
   32|       | *    get<POS>           - Type at position POS in the pack.
   33|       | *    first_t            - Type of first position in the pack.
   34|       | *    last_t             - Type of last position in the pack.
   35|       | *    select<Ps...>      - Create a new pack with types from selected positions.
   36|       | *
   37|       | *  Type manipulations:
   38|       | *    set<POS, T>        - Change type at position POS to T.
   39|       | *    push_front<Ts...>  - Add any number of types Ts to the front of the pack.
   40|       | *    push_back<Ts...>   - Add any number of types Ts to the back of the pack.
   41|       | *    pop                - Pack with first type missing.
   42|       | *    popN<N>            - Pack with first N types missing.
   43|       | *    shrink<N>          - Pack with ONLY first N types.
   44|       | *    resize<N,D>        - Resize pack to N types; if N greater than current size, pad with D.
   45|       | *    merge<P>           - Append all of pack P to the end of this pack.
   46|       | *    find_union<P>      - Join this pack to P, keeping only one of each type.
   47|       | *    reverse            - Reverse the order of types in this pack.
   48|       | *    rotate             - Move the first type in pack to the end.
   49|       | *
   50|       | *  Applications:
   51|       | *    apply<T>           - Take template T and apply these types as its arguments.
   52|       | *    to_function_t<T>   - Convert to a function type, with return type T and arg types from pack.
   53|       | *    filter<FILTER>     - Keep only those types, T, that can legally form FILTER<T> and does not
   54|       | *                         have a FILTER<T>::value == false.
   55|       | *    filter_out<FILTER> - Remove those types, T, that can legally form FILTER<T> and do not have
   56|       | *                         a FILTER<T>::value == false.
   57|       | *    find<FILTER>       - Convert to first type, T, that can legally form FILTER<T> and does not
   58|       | *                         have a FILTER<T>::value == false.
   59|       | *    wrap<WRAPPER>      - Convert to TypePack where all members are run through WRAPPER
   60|       | *
   61|       | *
   62|       | *  Developer notes:
   63|       | *    - GetIDPack could return an ValPack of ALL ID's for a type that appears more than once.
   64|       | */
   65|       |
   66|       |#ifndef EMP_TYPE_PACK_H
   67|       |#define EMP_TYPE_PACK_H
   68|       |
   69|       |#include "meta.hpp"
   70|       |
   71|       |namespace emp {
   72|       |
   73|       |  // Pre-declaration of TypePack
   74|       |  template <typename... Ts> struct TypePack;
   75|       |
   76|       |  // Create a null type for padding.
   77|       |  struct null_t {};
   78|       |
   79|       |  // Private helpers for TypePack
   80|       |  #ifndef DOXYGEN_SHOULD_SKIP_THIS
   81|       |  namespace internal {
   82|       |    // Create add N copies of the same type to the end of a TypePack.
   83|       |    template <typename START, typename T, int N>
   84|       |    struct tp_pad { using type = typename tp_pad<START,T,N-1>::type::template add<T>; };
   85|       |    template <typename START, typename T>
   86|       |    struct tp_pad<START, T,0> { using type = START; };
   87|       |
   88|       |    /// Helper for shifting a specified number of types to TypePack T1 from TypePack T2.
   89|       |    /// Example: to shrink, move the specified number of types to empty TypePack and return it.
   90|       |    ///          to merge, move all of one TypePack over to the other and return it.
   91|       |    template <int S, typename T1, typename T2>
   92|       |    struct tp_shift {
   93|       |      using move_t = typename T2::first_t;
   94|       |      using inc_t = typename T1::template add<move_t>;
   95|       |      using dec_t = typename T2::pop;
   96|       |      using type1 = typename tp_shift<S-1, inc_t, dec_t>::type1;
   97|       |      using type2 = typename tp_shift<S-1, inc_t, dec_t>::type2;
   98|       |    };
   99|       |    template <typename T1, typename T2>
  100|       |    struct tp_shift<0, T1, T2> {
  101|       |      using type1 = T1;
  102|       |      using type2 = T2;
  103|       |    };
  104|       |
  105|       |    // Filters create a TypePack with the element if the filter is true, empty if false.
  106|       |    template <typename T, bool> struct tp_filter1 { using type=TypePack<T>; };
  107|       |    template <typename T> struct tp_filter1<T,false> { using type=TypePack<>; };
  108|       |
  109|       |    /// Remove types from a TypePack that can't satisfy a filter.
  110|       |    template <typename T, template <typename...> class FILTER, int N>
  111|       |    struct tp_filter {
  112|       |      using cur_t = typename T::first_t;                                 // Isolate the first type
  113|       |      using other_tp = typename T::pop;                                  // Isolate remaining types
  114|       |      constexpr static bool cur_result = test_type<FILTER,cur_t>();      // Run filter of cur type
  115|       |      using cur_ftp = typename tp_filter1<cur_t, cur_result>::type;      // Use cur type if true
  116|       |      using other_ftp = typename tp_filter<other_tp, FILTER, N-1>::type; // Recurse
  117|       |      using type = typename cur_ftp::template merge< other_ftp >;        // Merge
  118|       |    };
  119|       |
  120|       |    template <typename T, template <typename...> class FILTER>
  121|       |      struct tp_filter<T,FILTER,0> { using type = TypePack<>; };
  122|       |
  123|       |    template <typename T, template <typename...> class FILTER>
  124|       |      using tp_filter_t = typename tp_filter<T,FILTER,T::SIZE>::type;
  125|       |
  126|       |    /// Remove types from a TypePack that DO satisfy a filter.
  127|       |    template <typename T, template <typename...> class FILTER, int N>
  128|       |    struct tp_filter_out {
  129|       |      using cur_t = typename T::first_t;                                 // Isolate the first type
  130|       |      using other_tp = typename T::pop;                                  // Isolate remaining types
  131|       |      constexpr static bool cur_result = test_type<FILTER,cur_t>();      // Run filter of cur type
  132|       |      using cur_ftp = typename tp_filter1<cur_t, !cur_result>::type;     // Use cur type if true
  133|       |      using other_ftp = typename tp_filter_out<other_tp, FILTER, N-1>::type; // Recurse
  134|       |      using type = typename cur_ftp::template merge< other_ftp >;        // Merge
  135|       |    };
  136|       |
  137|       |    template <typename T, template <typename...> class FILTER>
  138|       |      struct tp_filter_out<T,FILTER,0> { using type = TypePack<>; };
  139|       |
  140|       |    template <typename T, template <typename...> class FILTER>
  141|       |      using tp_filter_out_t = typename tp_filter_out<T,FILTER,T::SIZE>::type;
  142|       |
  143|       |    // Wrappers create a TypePack with the wrapped element if the filter is true, empty if false.
  144|       |    template <typename T, template <typename...> class W, bool> struct tp_wrap1
  145|       |      { using type=TypePack<W<T>>; };
  146|       |    template <typename T, template <typename...> class W> struct tp_wrap1<T,W,false>
  147|       |      { using type=TypePack<>; };
  148|       |
  149|       |    /// Wrap all types in a TypePack in a template; remove any that can't be wrapped.
  150|       |    template <typename T, template <typename...> class W, int N> struct tp_wrap {
  151|       |      using cur_t = typename T::first_t;                               // Isolate the first type
  152|       |      using other_tp = typename T::pop;                                // Isolate remaining types
  153|       |      constexpr static bool cur_result = test_type<W,cur_t>();         // Use wrap to filter cur type
  154|       |      using cur_ftp = typename tp_wrap1<cur_t, W, cur_result>::type;   // Use cur type if true
  155|       |      using other_ftp = typename tp_wrap<other_tp, W, N-1>::type;      // Recurse
  156|       |      using type = typename cur_ftp::template merge< other_ftp >;      // Merge
  157|       |    };
  158|       |    template <typename T, template <typename...> class W>
  159|       |      struct tp_wrap<T,W,0> { using type = TypePack<>; };
  160|       |
  161|       |    template <typename T, template <typename...> class W>
  162|       |      using tp_wrap_t = typename tp_wrap<T,W,T::SIZE>::type;
  163|       |
  164|       |    // Tools to build all combinations of types from a TypePack
  165|       |    template <size_t size,
  166|       |              typename ALL_OPTIONS,
  167|       |              typename CUR_OPTIONS>
  168|       |    struct all_combos {
  169|       |      // Determine combinations for one level smaller.
  170|       |      using next_combos = typename all_combos<size-1, ALL_OPTIONS, ALL_OPTIONS>::result_t;
  171|       |
  172|       |      using cur_type = typename CUR_OPTIONS::first_t;
  173|       |      using next_options = typename CUR_OPTIONS::pop;
  174|       |      template <typename T>
  175|       |      using push_wrap = typename T::template push_back<cur_type>;
  176|       |
  177|       |      // pushed_types is the set of currently-built types with the FIRST option added on.
  178|       |      using pushed_types = typename next_combos::template wrap<push_wrap>;
  179|       |
  180|       |      // other_results is the set of currently built types with the OTHER options added on.
  181|       |      using other_results = typename all_combos<size, ALL_OPTIONS, next_options>::result_t;
  182|       |
  183|       |      // cur_result_t is the merger of pushed_type and other types (i.e., ALL types added on)
  184|       |      using result_t = typename pushed_types::template merge<other_results>;
  185|       |    };
  186|       |
  187|       |    // When we are out of options return an empty pack.
  188|       |    template <size_t size,
  189|       |              typename ALL_OPTIONS>
  190|       |    struct all_combos<size, ALL_OPTIONS, emp::TypePack<>> {
  191|       |      using result_t = emp::TypePack<>;
  192|       |    };
  193|       |
  194|       |    // If we are out of combinations, just return the current answer.
  195|       |    template <typename ALL_OPTIONS>
  196|       |    struct all_combos<0,ALL_OPTIONS,ALL_OPTIONS> {
  197|       |      using result_t = emp::TypePack<emp::TypePack<>>;
  198|       |    };
  199|       |  }
  200|       |  #endif // DOXYGEN_SHOULD_SKIP_THIS
  201|       |
  202|       |  template <typename T, int N>
  203|       |  using TypePackFill = typename internal::tp_pad<TypePack<>,T,N>::type;
  204|       |
  205|       |  // Specialized TypePack with at least one type.
  206|       |  template <typename T1, typename... Ts>
  207|       |  struct TypePack<T1, Ts...> {
  208|       |    /// Return a bool indicating whether the specified type is present.
  209|       |    template <typename T> constexpr static bool Has() { return has_type<T,T1,Ts...>(); }
  210|       |
  211|       |    /// Count the number of instances of the specified type.
  212|       |    template <typename T> constexpr static size_t Count() { return count_type<T,T1,Ts...>(); }
  213|       |
  214|       |    /// Return the position of the specified type.
  215|       |    template <typename T> constexpr static int GetID() { return get_type_index<T,T1,Ts...>(); }
  216|       |
  217|       |    /// Return the position of the type of owner.
  218|       |    template <typename T>
  219|       |    constexpr static int GetID(const T &) { return get_type_index<T,T1,Ts...>(); }
  220|       |
  221|       |    /// Set to the number of types in this pack.
  222|       |    constexpr static int SIZE = 1+sizeof...(Ts);
  223|       |
  224|       |    /// Return the number of types in this pack.
  225|       |    constexpr static int GetSize() { return SIZE; }
  226|       |
  227|       |    /// Return bool indicating if there are any types in this pack.
  228|       |    constexpr static bool IsEmpty() { return false; }
  229|       |
  230|       |    /// Return bool indicating if all types in this pack are different from each other.
  231|       |    constexpr static bool IsUnique() { return has_unique_types<T1,Ts...>(); }
  232|       |
  233|       |    /// Count how many distinct types are in this pack.
  234|       |    constexpr static int CountUnique() { return make_unique::GetSize(); }
  235|       |
  236|       |    /// Get the type associated with a specified position in the pack.
  237|       |    template <int POS> using get = pack_id<POS, T1, Ts...>;
  238|       |
  239|       |    // Other type lookups
  240|       |
  241|       |    /// What is the full type of this TypePack?
  242|       |    // (mostly a helper for the code below)
  243|       |    using this_t = TypePack<T1, Ts...>;
  244|       |
  245|       |    /// What is the first type in this TypePack?
  246|       |    using first_t = T1;
  247|       |
  248|       |    /// What is the final type in this TypePack?
  249|       |    using last_t = last_type<T1,Ts...>;
  250|       |
  251|       |    // Modifications
  252|       |
  253|       |    /// Push a new type onto the front of this TypePack.
  254|       |    template <typename... T> using push_front = TypePack<T...,T1,Ts...>;
  255|       |
  256|       |    /// Push a new type onto the back of this TypePack.
  257|       |    template <typename... T> using push_back = TypePack<T1,Ts...,T...>;
  258|       |
  259|       |    /// Push a new type onto the back of this TypePack.
  260|       |    template <typename... T> using add = TypePack<T1,Ts...,T...>;    // Same as push_back_t...
  261|       |
  262|       |    /// Remove the first type from this TypePack and return the rest.
  263|       |    using pop = TypePack<Ts...>;
  264|       |    template <int N> using popN = typename internal::tp_shift<N, TypePack<>, this_t>::type2;
  265|       |
  266|       |    /// Reduce the size of this TypePack down to N.
  267|       |    template <int N> using shrink = typename internal::tp_shift<N, TypePack<>, this_t>::type1;
  268|       |
  269|       |    /// Add N new entries onto TypePack, all of provided type T.
  270|       |    template <typename T, int N=1> using pad = typename internal::tp_pad<this_t,T,N>::type;
  271|       |
  272|       |    /// Make this TypePack the specified size, padding with provided type T.
  273|       |    template <int N, typename DEFAULT=null_t>
  274|       |      using resize = typename pad<DEFAULT,(N>SIZE)?(N-SIZE):0>::template shrink<N>;
  275|       |
  276|       |    /// Join this TypePack with another TypePack (append)
  277|       |    template <typename IN> using merge = typename internal::tp_shift<IN::SIZE, this_t, IN>::type1;
  278|       |
  279|       |    /// Join this TypePack with another, keeping only one of each type.
  280|       |    template <typename IN> using find_union = typename internal::tp_shift<IN::SIZE, this_t, IN>::type1::make_unique;
  281|       |
  282|       |    /// Rearrange types in TypePack into reverse order.
  283|       |    using reverse = typename pop::reverse::template push_back<T1>;
  284|       |
  285|       |    /// Rotate types through typepack by the specified number of steps.
  286|       |    using rotate = typename pop::template push_back<T1>;
  287|       |
  288|       |    /// Set the type at the specified position to the new type provided.  Return as new TypePack.
  289|       |    template <int ID, typename T>
  290|       |      using set = typename shrink<ID>::template push_back<T>::template merge<popN<ID+1>>;
  291|       |
  292|       |    /// Choose a set of specific positions.  Return as new TypePack.
  293|       |    template <int... Ps> using select = TypePack< get<Ps>... >;
  294|       |
  295|       |    /// Convert to a function signature with a specified return type.
  296|       |    template <typename RETURN_T> using to_function_t = RETURN_T(T1,Ts...);
  297|       |
  298|       |    /// Apply to a specified template with TypePack as template arguments.
  299|       |    template <template <typename...> class TEMPLATE> using apply = TEMPLATE<T1, Ts...>;
  300|       |
  301|       |    /// Remove all types that cannot pass a filter.  Return as new TypePack.
  302|       |    template <template <typename...> class FILTER>
  303|       |    using filter = internal::tp_filter_t<this_t, FILTER>;
  304|       |
  305|       |    /// Remove all types that DO pass a filter.  Return as new TypePack.
  306|       |    template <template <typename...> class FILTER>
  307|       |    using filter_out = internal::tp_filter_out_t<this_t, FILTER>;
  308|       |
  309|       |    /// Return the first type that satisfies a filter.
  310|       |    template <template <typename...> class FILTER>
  311|       |    using find_t = typename internal::tp_filter_t<this_t, FILTER>::first_t;
  312|       |
  313|       |    template <typename M1>
  314|       |    struct type_matcher {
  315|       |      template <typename M2>
  316|       |      using is_match = std::is_same<M1,M2>;
  317|       |    };
  318|       |
  319|       |    /// Remove all instances of a given type.
  320|       |    template <typename T>
  321|       |    using remove_t = internal::tp_filter_out_t<this_t, type_matcher<T>::template is_match>;
  322|       |
  323|       |    /// Remove all duplicates within a TypePack
  324|       |    using make_unique = typename remove_t<first_t>::make_unique::template push_front<first_t>;
  325|       |
  326|       |    /// Wrap all types in a specified wrapper template.
  327|       |    template <template <typename...> class WRAPPER>
  328|       |    using wrap = internal::tp_wrap_t<this_t, WRAPPER>;
  329|       |
  330|       |    /// Figure out all of the combinations of types possible.
  331|       |    template <size_t COMBO_SIZE>
  332|       |    using make_combos = typename internal::all_combos<COMBO_SIZE,this_t,this_t>::result_t;
  333|       |  };

   64|       |size_t sidebyside_hash(const size_t top, const size_t bottom) {

   67|       |  // exclude int sign bit
   68|       |  constexpr size_t avail_size = int_size - 1;
   69|       |  constexpr size_t bottom_size = avail_size * BottomFrac::num / BottomFrac::den;
   70|       |  [[maybe_unused]] constexpr size_t top_size = avail_size - bottom_size;
   71|       |
   72|       |  // bounds checking
   73|       |  uitsl_assert(std::bitset<top_size>(top).to_ullong() == top, top);
   74|       |  uitsl_assert(std::bitset<bottom_size>(bottom).to_ullong() == bottom, bottom);
   75|       |
   76|       |  const auto bottom_bits = std::bitset<int_size>( bottom );
   77|       |  const auto top_bits = std::bitset<int_size>( top ) << bottom_size;
   78|       |
   79|       |  const auto res = top_bits | bottom_bits;
   80|       |
   81|       |  return res.to_ullong();
   82|       |

  103|       |  ///  Middle Square Weyl Sequence: A versatile and non-patterned pseudo-random-number
  104|       |  ///  generator.
  105|       |  ///  Based on: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle-square_method

  111|       |    uint64_t original_seed = 0;               ///< Seed to start sequence; initialized weyl_state
  112|       |
  113|       |    // Members & functions for stat functions
  114|       |    double expRV = 0.0;    ///< Exponential Random Variable for the randNormal function
  115|       |

  122|       |    /// Returns a random number [0, RAND_CAP)

  125|    202|      value += (weyl_state += STEP_SIZE);   // Take a step in the Weyl sequence
  126|    202|      value = (value>>32) | (value<<32);    // Return the middle of the value

  128|    202|    }

  272|       |    /// Generate a random variable drawn from a unit normal distribution.
  273|      0|    double GetRandNormal() {
  274|      0|      // Draw from a Unit Normal Dist
  275|      0|      // Using Rejection Method and saving of initial exponential random variable
  276|      0|      double expRV2;
  277|      0|      while (1) {
  278|      0|        expRV2 = -log(GetDouble());
  279|      0|        expRV -= (expRV2-1)*(expRV2-1)/2;
  280|      0|        if (expRV > 0) break;
  281|      0|        expRV = -log(GetDouble());
  282|      0|      }
  283|      0|      if (P(.5)) return expRV2;
  284|      0|      return -expRV2;
  285|      0|    }

  292|       |    /// @return A random variable drawn from a pareto distribution.
  293|       |    /// @param alpha Shape parameter (default 1).
  294|       |    /// @param lower_bound Lower bound (default 1).
  295|       |    /// @param upper_bound Upper bound (default infinity, unbounded).
  296|       |    /// See <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pareto_distribution>
  297|       |    inline double GetRandPareto(
  298|       |      const double alpha=1.0,
  299|       |      const double lower_bound=1.0,
  300|       |      const double upper_bound=std::numeric_limits<double>::infinity()
  301|      0|    ) {
  302|      0|      emp_assert( alpha > 0.0, alpha );
  303|      0|      emp_assert( lower_bound > 0.0, lower_bound );
  304|      0|      emp_assert( lower_bound <= upper_bound, lower_bound, upper_bound );
  305|      0|      if (lower_bound == upper_bound) return lower_bound;
  306|      0|      // uses inverse transform sampling method
  307|      0|      // see https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inverse_transform_sampling
  308|      0|      // and https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pareto_distribution
  309|      0|
  310|      0|      // see https://gist.github.com/mmore500/b6de0158a8851ce6e3d65105ed35197f
  311|      0|      // for validation and testing
  312|      0|      // url has also been archived on the archive.org wayback machine
  313|      0|
  314|      0|      const double unif_lb = std::pow(lower_bound / upper_bound, alpha);
  315|      0|      constexpr double unif_ub = 1.0;
  316|      0|      const double unif_sample = GetDouble(unif_lb, unif_ub);
  317|      0|
  318|      0|      return lower_bound / std::pow(unif_sample, 1.0/alpha);
  319|      0|    }

  337|       |    /// @return A random variable drawn from a distribution with symmetric
  338|       |    /// pareto tails extending both positively and negatively from zero.
  339|       |    /// @param alpha Shape parameter (default 1).
  340|       |    /// @param lambda Scale parameter (default 1).
  341|       |    /// @param lower_bound Lower bound (default -infinity, unbounded).
  342|       |    /// @param upper_bound Upper bound (default infinity, unbounded).
  343|       |    /// Inspired by <https://doi.org/10.1109/TBC.2004.834013>
  344|       |    inline double GetRandZeroSymmetricPareto(
  345|       |      const double alpha=1.0,
  346|       |      const double lambda=1.0,
  347|       |      const double lower_bound=-std::numeric_limits<double>::infinity(),
  348|       |      const double upper_bound=std::numeric_limits<double>::infinity()
  349|      0|    ) {
  350|      0|      emp_assert( alpha > 0.0, alpha );
  351|      0|      emp_assert( lower_bound <= 0, lower_bound );
  352|      0|      emp_assert( upper_bound >= 0, upper_bound );
  353|      0|
  354|      0|      // see https://gist.github.com/mmore500/2b95ef3279e49fc1780b3b319c35083a
  355|      0|      // for derivations and validation
  356|      0|      // url has also been archived on the archive.org wayback machine
  357|      0|
  358|      0|      const double n = std::min(std::abs(lower_bound), upper_bound);
  359|      0|      const double m = std::max(std::abs(lower_bound), upper_bound);
  360|      0|
  361|      0|      // probability of picking on the skinny side of zero
  362|      0|      const double p_skinny_side
  363|      0|        = impl::calc_p_zero_symmetric_pareto_skinny_side(
  364|      0|          alpha, lambda, m, n
  365|      0|        );
  366|      0|
  367|      0|      // is the fat side of the distribution negative?
  368|      0|      const bool skinny_side_is_positive = std::abs(lower_bound) > upper_bound;
  369|      0|
  370|      0|      // pick which side of zero to draw from then do draw with a lomax dist
  371|      0|      if (P(p_skinny_side) == skinny_side_is_positive) {
  372|      0|        // draw from positive side
  373|      0|        return GetRandLomax(alpha, lambda, upper_bound);
  374|      0|      } else {
  375|      0|        // draw from negative side
  376|      0|        return -GetRandLomax(alpha, lambda, std::abs(lower_bound));
  377|      0|      }
  378|      0|
  379|      0|    }

  390|      0|    inline uint32_t GetRandPoisson(const double mean) {
  391|      0|      // Draw from a Poisson Dist with mean; if cannot calculate, return UINT_MAX.
  392|      0|      // Uses Rejection Method

  394|      0|      if (a <= 0) return UINT_MAX; // cannot calculate, so return UINT_MAX

  396|      0|      double u = GetDouble();
  397|      0|      while (u >= a) {
  398|      0|        u *= GetDouble();
  399|      0|        ++k;
  400|      0|      }
  401|      0|      return k;
  402|      0|    }

   56|       |    /// Produce a vector that spreads values evenly across the range.
   57|       |    emp::vector<T> Spread(size_t s) const {
   58|       |      emp_assert(s >= 1);
   59|       |      emp::vector<T> out(s);
   60|       |      out[0] = lower;
   61|       |      if (s > 1) {
   62|       |        T range = upper - lower;
   63|       |        for (size_t i = 1; i < s; i++) {
   64|       |          out[i] = lower + (T) ((((double) i) / (double)(s-1)) * range);
   65|       |        }
   66|       |      }
   67|       |      return out;
   68|       |    }

  218|       |  /// A fast (O(log p)) integral-power command.
  219|       |  template <typename T>
  220|       |  static constexpr T PowIntImpl(T base, T p) {
  221|       |    if (p <= 0) return 1;
  222|       |    if (p & 1) return base * PowIntImpl(base, p-1); // Odd exponent: strip one mulitple off and recurse.
  223|       |    return emp::Square( PowIntImpl(base,p/2) );          // Even exponent: calc for half and square result.
  224|       |  }
  225|       |  } // namespace internal
  226|       |  #endif // DOXYGEN_SHOULD_SKIP_THIS

  242|       |  /// A fast method for calculating exponents on doubles.
  243|      0|  static constexpr double PowDoubleImpl(double base, double exp) {
  244|      0|    // Normally, convert to a base of 2 and then use Pow2.
  245|      0|    // If base is negative, we don't want to deal with imaginary numbers, so use IntPow.
  246|      0|    return (base > 0)
  247|      0|      ? emp::Pow2(emp::Log2(base) * exp)
  248|      0|      : emp::IntPow(base,exp);
  249|      0|  }

  258|       |  /// A fast method for calculating exponents on doubles or integral types.
  259|       |  /// Uses if constexpr to work around compiler bug in Emscripten (issue #296).
  260|       |  template<typename T>
  261|       |  static constexpr T Pow(
  262|       |    T base, typename internal::identity<T>::type exp
  263|       |  ) {
  264|       |    // TODO cpp20, C++20 replace with std::is_constant_evaluated
  265|       |    // adapted from https://stackoverflow.com/a/62610143
  266|       |    // exclude clang versions with compiler bug https://reviews.llvm.org/D35190
  267|       |    #if defined(__clang__) && __clang_major__>=9 || defined(__GNUC__) && !defined(__clang__)
  268|       |    // if base is not known at compile time, use std::pow which is faster
  269|       |    if ( !__builtin_constant_p( base ) ) return std::pow(base, exp);
  270|       |    // otherwise, use constexpr-friendly implementations
  271|       |    else
  272|       |    #endif
  273|       |    if constexpr( std::is_integral<T>::value ){
  274|       |      return internal::PowIntImpl(base, exp);
  275|       |    } else return internal::PowDoubleImpl(base, exp);
  276|       |  }

  299|       |  /// A version of Min that allows a variable number of inputs to be compared.
  300|       |  template <typename T> const T & Min(std::initializer_list<const T&> lst) {
  301|       |    emp_assert(lst.size > 0); // Nothing to return if nothing in the list!
  302|       |    auto min_found = lst.begin();
  303|       |    for (auto it = lst.begin()+1; it < lst.end(); it++) {
  304|       |      if (*it < *min_found) min_found = it;
  305|       |    }
  306|       |    return *min_found;
  307|       |  }

  313|       |    for (auto it = lst.begin()+1; it < lst.end(); it++) {
  314|       |      if (*it > *max_found) max_found = it;
  315|       |    }

  363|       |  /// Gives incorrect answer for exp outside of representable range of double
  364|       |  /// exponent field.
  365|      0|  inline constexpr double exp2_overflow_unsafe(const int64_t exp) {
  366|      0|    // benchmarking result https://quick-bench.com/q/8Q7UMP35zo3t2C70HvEz4AzyWVE

  368|      0|    emp_assert(exp > -1023, exp);

  370|      0|    static_assert(sizeof(double) == sizeof(uint64_t));
  371|      0|    static_assert(std::numeric_limits<double>::is_iec559);
  372|      0|
  373|      0|    union {
  374|      0|      uint64_t i;
  375|      0|      double d;
  376|      0|    } result{};

  378|      0|    emp_assert(result.d > 0.0, exp);

  792|       |// Prevent signed-unsigned mismatch:
  793|       |
  794|      0|#define span_sizeof(T)  static_cast<extent_t>( sizeof(T) )
  795|       |
  796|       |template< class T >
  797|       |inline span_constexpr size_t to_size( T size )
  798|    202|{
  799|    202|    return static_cast<size_t>( size );
  800|    202|}
  801|       |
  802|       |//
  803|       |// [views.span] - A view over a contiguous, single-dimension sequence of objects
  804|       |//
  805|       |template< class T, extent_t Extent /*= dynamic_extent*/ >
  806|       |class span
  807|       |{
  808|       |public:
  809|       |    // constants and types
  810|       |
  811|       |    typedef T element_type;
  812|       |    typedef typename std11::remove_cv< T >::type value_type;
  813|       |
  814|       |    typedef T &       reference;
  815|       |    typedef T *       pointer;
  816|       |    typedef T const * const_pointer;
  817|       |    typedef T const & const_reference;
  818|       |
  819|       |    typedef size_t    size_type;
  820|       |    typedef extent_t  extent_type;
  821|       |
  822|       |    typedef pointer        iterator;
  823|       |    typedef const_pointer  const_iterator;
  824|       |
  825|       |    typedef std::ptrdiff_t difference_type;
  826|       |
  827|       |    typedef std::reverse_iterator< iterator >       reverse_iterator;
  828|       |    typedef std::reverse_iterator< const_iterator > const_reverse_iterator;
  829|       |
  830|       |//    static constexpr extent_type extent = Extent;
  831|       |    enum { extent = Extent };
  832|       |
  833|       |    // 26.7.3.2 Constructors, copy, and assignment [span.cons]
  834|       |
  835|       |#if span_HAVE( DEFAULT_FUNCTION_TEMPLATE_ARG )
  836|       |    span_REQUIRES_0(
  837|       |        ( Extent == 0 ) ||
  838|       |        ( Extent == dynamic_extent )
  839|       |    )
  840|       |#endif
  841|       |    span_constexpr span() span_noexcept
  842|       |        : data_( span_nullptr )
  843|       |        , size_( 0 )
  844|       |    {
  845|       |        // span_EXPECTS( data() == span_nullptr );
  846|       |        // span_EXPECTS( size() == 0 );
  847|       |    }
  848|       |
  849|       |    span_constexpr_exp span( pointer ptr, size_type count )
  850|      4|        : data_( ptr )
  851|      4|        , size_( count )
  852|      4|    {
  853|      4|        span_EXPECTS(
  854|      4|            ( ptr == span_nullptr && count == 0 ) ||
  855|      4|            ( ptr != span_nullptr && count >= 0 )
  856|      4|        );
  857|      4|    }
  858|       |
  859|       |    span_constexpr_exp span( pointer firstElem, pointer lastElem )
  860|       |        : data_( firstElem )
  861|       |        , size_( to_size( std::distance( firstElem, lastElem ) ) )
  862|       |    {
  863|       |        span_EXPECTS(
  864|       |            std::distance( firstElem, lastElem ) >= 0
  865|       |        );
  866|       |    }
  867|       |
  868|       |    template< std::size_t N
  869|       |#if span_HAVE( DEFAULT_FUNCTION_TEMPLATE_ARG )
  870|       |        span_REQUIRES_T((
  871|       |            (Extent == dynamic_extent || Extent == static_cast<extent_t>(N))
  872|       |            && std::is_convertible< value_type(*)[], element_type(*)[] >::value
  873|       |        ))
  874|       |#endif
  875|       |    >
  876|       |    span_constexpr span( element_type ( &arr )[ N ] ) span_noexcept
  877|       |        : data_( span_ADDRESSOF( arr[0] ) )
  878|       |        , size_( N  )
  879|       |    {}
  880|       |
  881|       |#if span_HAVE( ARRAY )
  882|       |
  883|       |    template< std::size_t N
  884|       |# if span_HAVE( DEFAULT_FUNCTION_TEMPLATE_ARG )
  885|       |        span_REQUIRES_T((
  886|       |            (Extent == dynamic_extent || Extent == static_cast<extent_t>(N))
  887|       |            && std::is_convertible< value_type(*)[], element_type(*)[] >::value
  888|       |        ))
  889|       |# endif
  890|       |    >
  891|       |# if span_FEATURE( CONSTRUCTION_FROM_STDARRAY_ELEMENT_TYPE )
  892|       |        span_constexpr span( std::array< element_type, N > & arr ) span_noexcept
  893|       |# else
  894|       |        span_constexpr span( std::array< value_type, N > & arr ) span_noexcept
  895|       |# endif
  896|       |        : data_( arr.data() )
  897|       |        , size_( to_size( arr.size() ) )
  898|       |    {}
  899|       |
  900|       |    template< std::size_t N
  901|       |# if span_HAVE( DEFAULT_FUNCTION_TEMPLATE_ARG )
  902|       |        span_REQUIRES_T((
  903|       |            (Extent == dynamic_extent || Extent == static_cast<extent_t>(N))
  904|       |            && std::is_convertible< value_type(*)[], element_type(*)[] >::value
  905|       |        ))
  906|       |# endif
  907|       |    >
  908|       |    span_constexpr span( std::array< value_type, N> const & arr ) span_noexcept
  909|       |        : data_( arr.data() )
  910|       |        , size_( to_size( arr.size() ) )
  911|       |    {}
  912|       |
  913|       |#endif // span_HAVE( ARRAY )
  914|       |
  915|       |#if span_HAVE( CONSTRAINED_SPAN_CONTAINER_CTOR )
  916|       |    template< class Container
  917|       |        span_REQUIRES_T((
  918|       |            detail::is_compatible_container< Container, element_type >::value
  919|       |        ))
  920|       |    >
  921|       |    span_constexpr span( Container & cont )
  922|       |        : data_( std17::data( cont ) )
  923|       |        , size_( to_size( std17::size( cont ) ) )
  924|       |    {}
  925|       |
  926|       |    template< class Container
  927|       |        span_REQUIRES_T((
  928|       |            std::is_const< element_type >::value
  929|       |            && detail::is_compatible_container< Container, element_type >::value
  930|       |        ))
  931|       |    >
  932|       |    span_constexpr span( Container const & cont )
  933|       |        : data_( std17::data( cont ) )
  934|       |        , size_( to_size( std17::size( cont ) ) )
  935|       |    {}
  936|       |
  937|       |#endif // span_HAVE( CONSTRAINED_SPAN_CONTAINER_CTOR )
  938|       |
  939|       |#if span_FEATURE( WITH_CONTAINER )
  940|       |
  941|       |    template< class Container >
  942|       |    span_constexpr span( with_container_t, Container & cont )
  943|       |        : data_( cont.size() == 0 ? span_nullptr : span_ADDRESSOF( cont[0] ) )
  944|       |        , size_( to_size( cont.size() ) )
  945|       |    {}
  946|       |
  947|       |    template< class Container >
  948|       |    span_constexpr span( with_container_t, Container const & cont )
  949|       |        : data_( cont.size() == 0 ? span_nullptr : const_cast<pointer>( span_ADDRESSOF( cont[0] ) ) )
  950|       |        , size_( to_size( cont.size() ) )
  951|       |    {}
  952|       |#endif
  953|       |
  954|       |#if span_HAVE( IS_DEFAULT )
  955|       |    span_constexpr span( span const & other ) span_noexcept = default;
  956|       |
  957|       |    ~span() span_noexcept = default;
  958|       |
  959|       |    span_constexpr14 span & operator=( span const & other ) span_noexcept = default;
  960|       |#else
  961|       |    span_constexpr span( span const & other ) span_noexcept
  962|       |        : data_( other.data_ )
  963|       |        , size_( other.size_ )
  964|       |    {}
  965|       |
  966|       |    ~span() span_noexcept
  967|       |    {}
  968|       |
  969|       |    span_constexpr14 span & operator=( span const & other ) span_noexcept
  970|       |    {
  971|       |        data_ = other.data_;
  972|       |        size_ = other.size_;
  973|       |
  974|       |        return *this;
  975|       |    }
  976|       |#endif
  977|       |
  978|       |    template< class OtherElementType, extent_type OtherExtent
  979|       |#if span_HAVE( DEFAULT_FUNCTION_TEMPLATE_ARG )
  980|       |        span_REQUIRES_T((
  981|       |            (Extent == dynamic_extent || Extent == OtherExtent)
  982|       |            && std::is_convertible<OtherElementType(*)[], element_type(*)[]>::value
  983|       |        ))
  984|       |#endif
  985|       |    >
  986|       |    span_constexpr_exp span( span<OtherElementType, OtherExtent> const & other ) span_noexcept
  987|       |        : data_( reinterpret_cast<pointer>( other.data() ) )
  988|       |        , size_( other.size() )
  989|       |    {
  990|       |        span_EXPECTS( OtherExtent == dynamic_extent || other.size() == to_size(OtherExtent) );
  991|       |    }
  992|       |
  993|       |    // 26.7.3.3 Subviews [span.sub]
  994|       |
  995|       |    template< extent_type Count >
  996|       |    span_constexpr_exp span< element_type, Count >
  997|       |    first() const
  998|       |    {
  999|       |        span_EXPECTS( 0 <= Count && Count <= size() );
 1000|       |
 1001|       |        return span< element_type, Count >( data(), Count );
 1002|       |    }
 1003|       |
 1004|       |    template< extent_type Count >
 1005|       |    span_constexpr_exp span< element_type, Count >
 1006|       |    last() const
 1007|       |    {
 1008|       |        span_EXPECTS( 0 <= Count && Count <= size() );
 1009|       |
 1010|       |        return span< element_type, Count >( data() + (size() - Count), Count );
 1011|       |    }
 1012|       |
 1013|       |#if span_HAVE( DEFAULT_FUNCTION_TEMPLATE_ARG )
 1014|       |    template< size_type Offset, extent_type Count = dynamic_extent >
 1015|       |#else
 1016|       |    template< size_type Offset, extent_type Count /*= dynamic_extent*/ >
 1017|       |#endif
 1018|       |    span_constexpr_exp span< element_type, Count >
 1019|       |    subspan() const
 1020|       |    {
 1021|       |        span_EXPECTS(
 1022|       |            ( 0 <= Offset && Offset <= size() ) &&
 1023|       |            ( Count == dynamic_extent || (0 <= Count && Count + Offset <= size()) )
 1024|       |        );
 1025|       |
 1026|       |        return span< element_type, Count >(
 1027|       |            data() + Offset, Count != dynamic_extent ? Count : (Extent != dynamic_extent ? Extent - Offset : size() - Offset) );
 1028|       |    }
 1029|       |
 1030|       |    span_constexpr_exp span< element_type, dynamic_extent >
 1031|       |    first( size_type count ) const
 1032|       |    {
 1033|       |        span_EXPECTS( 0 <= count && count <= size() );
 1034|       |
 1035|       |        return span< element_type, dynamic_extent >( data(), count );
 1036|       |    }
 1037|       |
 1038|       |    span_constexpr_exp span< element_type, dynamic_extent >
 1039|       |    last( size_type count ) const
 1040|       |    {
 1041|       |        span_EXPECTS( 0 <= count && count <= size() );
 1042|       |
 1043|       |        return span< element_type, dynamic_extent >( data() + ( size() - count ), count );
 1044|       |    }
 1045|       |
 1046|       |    span_constexpr_exp span< element_type, dynamic_extent >
 1047|       |    subspan( size_type offset, size_type count = static_cast<size_type>(dynamic_extent) ) const
 1048|       |    {
 1049|       |        span_EXPECTS(
 1050|       |            ( ( 0 <= offset  && offset <= size() ) ) &&
 1051|       |            ( count == static_cast<size_type>(dynamic_extent) || ( 0 <= count && offset + count <= size() ) )
 1052|       |        );
 1053|       |
 1054|       |        return span< element_type, dynamic_extent >(
 1055|       |            data() + offset, count == static_cast<size_type>(dynamic_extent) ? size() - offset : count );
 1056|       |    }
 1057|       |
 1058|       |    // 26.7.3.4 Observers [span.obs]
 1059|       |
 1060|       |    span_constexpr size_type size() const span_noexcept
 1061|    404|    {
 1062|    404|        return size_;
 1063|    404|    }
 1064|       |
 1065|       |    span_constexpr std::ptrdiff_t ssize() const span_noexcept
 1066|       |    {
 1067|       |        return static_cast<std::ptrdiff_t>( size_ );
 1068|       |    }
 1069|       |
 1070|       |    span_constexpr size_type size_bytes() const span_noexcept
 1071|    202|    {
 1072|    202|        return size() * to_size( sizeof( element_type ) );
 1073|    202|    }
 1074|       |
 1075|       |    span_nodiscard span_constexpr bool empty() const span_noexcept
 1076|       |    {
 1077|       |        return size() == 0;
 1078|       |    }
 1079|       |
 1080|       |    // 26.7.3.5 Element access [span.elem]
 1081|       |
 1082|       |    span_constexpr_exp reference operator[]( size_type idx ) const
 1083|    202|    {
 1084|    202|        span_EXPECTS( 0 <= idx && idx < size() );
 1085|       |
 1086|    202|        return *( data() + idx );
 1087|    202|    }
 1088|       |
 1089|       |#if span_FEATURE( MEMBER_CALL_OPERATOR )
 1090|       |    span_deprecated("replace operator() with operator[]")
 1091|       |
 1092|       |    span_constexpr_exp reference operator()( size_type idx ) const
 1093|       |    {
 1094|       |        span_EXPECTS( 0 <= idx && idx < size() );
 1095|       |
 1096|       |        return *( data() + idx );
 1097|       |    }
 1098|       |#endif
 1099|       |
 1100|       |#if span_FEATURE( MEMBER_AT )
 1101|       |    span_constexpr14 reference at( size_type idx ) const
 1102|       |    {
 1103|       |#if span_CONFIG( NO_EXCEPTIONS )
 1104|       |        return this->operator[]( idx );
 1105|       |#else
 1106|       |        if ( idx < 0 || size() <= idx )
 1107|       |        {
 1108|       |            detail::throw_out_of_range( idx, size() );
 1109|       |        }
 1110|       |        return *( data() + idx );
 1111|       |#endif
 1112|       |    }
 1113|       |#endif
 1114|       |
 1115|       |    span_constexpr pointer data() const span_noexcept
 1116|    202|    {
 1117|    202|        return data_;
 1118|    202|    }
 1119|       |
 1120|       |#if span_FEATURE( MEMBER_BACK_FRONT )
 1121|       |
 1122|       |    span_constexpr_exp reference front() const span_noexcept
 1123|       |    {
 1124|       |        span_EXPECTS( ! empty() );
 1125|       |
 1126|       |        return *data();
 1127|       |    }
 1128|       |
 1129|       |    span_constexpr_exp reference back() const span_noexcept
 1130|       |    {
 1131|       |        span_EXPECTS( ! empty() );
 1132|       |
 1133|       |        return *( data() + size() - 1 );
 1134|       |    }
 1135|       |
 1136|       |#endif
 1137|       |
 1138|       |    // xx.x.x.x Modifiers [span.modifiers]
 1139|       |
 1140|       |#if span_FEATURE( MEMBER_SWAP )
 1141|       |
 1142|       |    span_constexpr14 void swap( span & other ) span_noexcept
 1143|       |    {
 1144|       |        using std::swap;
 1145|       |        swap( data_, other.data_ );
 1146|       |        swap( size_, other.size_ );
 1147|       |    }
 1148|       |#endif
 1149|       |
 1150|       |    // 26.7.3.6 Iterator support [span.iterators]
 1151|       |
 1152|       |    span_constexpr iterator begin() const span_noexcept
 1153|       |    {
 1154|       |#if span_CPP11_OR_GREATER
 1155|       |        return { data() };
 1156|       |#else
 1157|       |        return iterator( data() );
 1158|       |#endif
 1159|       |    }
 1160|       |
 1161|       |    span_constexpr iterator end() const span_noexcept
 1162|       |    {
 1163|       |#if span_CPP11_OR_GREATER
 1164|       |        return { data() + size() };
 1165|       |#else
 1166|       |        return iterator( data() + size() );
 1167|       |#endif
 1168|       |    }
 1169|       |
 1170|       |    span_constexpr const_iterator cbegin() const span_noexcept
 1171|       |    {
 1172|       |#if span_CPP11_OR_GREATER
 1173|       |        return { data() };
 1174|       |#else
 1175|       |        return const_iterator( data() );
 1176|       |#endif
 1177|       |    }
 1178|       |
 1179|       |    span_constexpr const_iterator cend() const span_noexcept
 1180|       |    {
 1181|       |#if span_CPP11_OR_GREATER
 1182|       |        return { data() + size() };
 1183|       |#else
 1184|       |        return const_iterator( data() + size() );
 1185|       |#endif
 1186|       |    }
 1187|       |
 1188|       |    span_constexpr reverse_iterator rbegin() const span_noexcept
 1189|       |    {
 1190|       |        return reverse_iterator( end() );
 1191|       |    }
 1192|       |
 1193|       |    span_constexpr reverse_iterator rend() const span_noexcept
 1194|       |    {
 1195|       |        return reverse_iterator( begin() );
 1196|       |    }
 1197|       |
 1198|       |    span_constexpr const_reverse_iterator crbegin() const span_noexcept
 1199|       |    {
 1200|       |        return const_reverse_iterator ( cend() );
 1201|       |    }
 1202|       |
 1203|       |    span_constexpr const_reverse_iterator crend() const span_noexcept
 1204|       |    {
 1205|       |        return const_reverse_iterator( cbegin() );
 1206|       |    }
 1207|       |
 1208|       |private:
 1209|       |    pointer   data_;
 1210|       |    size_type size_;
 1211|       |};

 1239|       |template< class T1, extent_t E1, class T2, extent_t E2  >
 1240|       |inline span_constexpr bool same( span<T1,E1> const & l, span<T2,E2> const & r ) span_noexcept
 1241|       |{
 1242|       |    return std11::is_same<T1, T2>::value
 1243|       |        && l.size() == r.size()
 1244|       |        && static_cast<void const*>( l.data() ) == r.data();
 1245|       |}
 1246|       |
 1247|       |#endif
 1248|       |
 1249|       |template< class T1, extent_t E1, class T2, extent_t E2  >
 1250|       |inline span_constexpr bool operator==( span<T1,E1> const & l, span<T2,E2> const & r )
 1251|       |{
 1252|       |    return
 1253|       |#if span_FEATURE( SAME )
 1254|       |        same( l, r ) ||
 1255|       |#endif
 1256|       |        ( l.size() == r.size() && std::equal( l.begin(), l.end(), r.begin() ) );
 1257|       |}

 1594|       |class tuple_size< nonstd::span<ElementType, nonstd::dynamic_extent> >;
 1595|       |
 1596|       |// std::tuple_element<>:
 1597|       |
 1598|       |template< size_t I, typename ElementType, nonstd::span_lite::extent_t Extent >
 1599|       |class tuple_element< I, nonstd::span<ElementType, Extent> >
 1600|       |{
 1601|       |public:
 1602|       |#if span_HAVE( STATIC_ASSERT )
 1603|       |    static_assert( Extent != nonstd::dynamic_extent && I < Extent, "tuple_element<I,span>: dynamic extent or index out of range" );
 1604|       |#endif
 1605|       |    using type = ElementType;
 1606|       |};
 1607|       |
 1608|       |// std::get<>(), 2 variants:
 1609|       |
 1610|       |template< size_t I, typename ElementType, nonstd::span_lite::extent_t Extent >
 1611|       |span_constexpr ElementType & get( nonstd::span<ElementType, Extent> & spn ) span_noexcept
 1612|       |{
 1613|       |#if span_HAVE( STATIC_ASSERT )
 1614|       |    static_assert( Extent != nonstd::dynamic_extent && I < Extent, "get<>(span): dynamic extent or index out of range" );
 1615|       |#endif
 1616|       |    return spn[I];
 1617|       |}
 1618|       |
 1619|       |template< size_t I, typename ElementType, nonstd::span_lite::extent_t Extent >
 1620|       |span_constexpr ElementType const & get( nonstd::span<ElementType, Extent> const & spn ) span_noexcept
 1621|       |{
 1622|       |#if span_HAVE( STATIC_ASSERT )
 1623|       |    static_assert( Extent != nonstd::dynamic_extent && I < Extent, "get<>(span): dynamic extent or index out of range" );
 1624|       |#endif
 1625|       |    return spn[I];
 1626|       |}
 1627|       |
 1628|       |} // end namespace std
 1629|       |
 1630|       |#endif // span_HAVE( STRUCT_BINDING )
 1631|       |
 1632|       |#if ! span_USES_STD_SPAN
 1633|       |span_RESTORE_WARNINGS()
 1634|       |#endif  // span_USES_STD_SPAN
 1635|       |
